Below is a fully specified protocol for the top two items so we can run immediately.
1) ψ-Modulated Clock Drift (Priority 1)
Objective
Detect a reproducible phase/frequency deviation between two nominally identical clocks when one clock’s signal path includes the “activator” optical stack (or co-located field), while the other serves as control.
Apparatus

2× disciplined oscillators at 10 MHz (OCXO/TCXO; GPSDO preferred for stability baselining).
Time-interval counter or phase comparator (e.g., 10 ps resolution class) logging to PC.
RF cabling: matched length, same connectors; one path routed through/adjacent to activator stack.
Optical “activator” assembly: cracked/strained lens + adjustable polarizers on a rigid mount with micrometer translation; optional Faraday cage around both paths.
Environmental controls: foam enclosure, temperature probe, power line conditioner/UPS.
Alignment & Calibration
Warm-up oscillators ≥30 min (OCXO) or ≥2 h (GPSDO).
Match cable lengths to within 1 cm; measure baseline delay with stack absent.
Swap cables between channels and repeat to estimate channel asymmetry; store both baselines.
Place the activator next to only one path; ensure no mechanical strain on cable.
Verify no measurable EM leakage from any LEDs/lasers in the activator (SDR sweep).
Protocol
Phase-difference logging at 1–10 Hz for 3× 30-minute blocks per condition:
A) Control (no activator near either path)
B) Activator near Path 1
C) Activator near Path 2 (role-swap)
D) Sham: inert glass element replacing activator
Randomize order: e.g., A, C, D, B to reduce drift bias.
Record temperature and supply voltage continuously.
Measurements & Analysis
Compute instantaneous frequency offset via phase-derivative; detrend slow thermal drift.
Estimate effect size = mean(Δf|Activator) − mean(Δf|Control).
Paired permutation test across blocks; report Cohen’s d with 95% CI.
Check symmetry: effect should follow the path that hosts the activator (B vs C).
Pre-register a smallest effect size of interest (e.g., 1×10⁻¹¹ fractional) to avoid p-hacking.
Failure Modes / Controls
Thermal gradients → put both cables in same foam channel; use dummy heater to equalize.
Ground loops → isolation transformer or battery supply; verify with scope.
Observer bias → blind the operator to condition labels where possible.
Go/No-Go Criterion
Proceed to week-long run only if short-run shows a consistent signed effect with p<0.01 and path-swap agreement.
2) ψ-Null Photometry (Priority 2)
Objective
Test for intensity minima or contrast anomalies not predicted by Malus’s law when using the cracked-lens + polarizer stack under specific orientations and micro-translations.
Apparatus

Stable LED source + pinhole + collimator (or laser with diffuser to reduce speckle).
Two high-quality linear polarizers (analyzer on rotation stage, 0.1° resolution).
Cracked/strained lens fragment on XY micrometer stage; optional quarter-wave plate.
Photodiode + TIA (≥100 kHz bandwidth), 16-bit DAQ; baffle tube to suppress stray light.
Vibration-isolated rail; black shroud; temperature probe.
Alignment
Baseline: Measure I(θ) with just crossed/parallel polarizers; fit Malus I=I₀cos²θ to confirm linear regime.
Insert cracked lens between polarizers; scan θ in 0.5° steps, record I(θ).
At fixed θ near expected anomaly (from notes), raster the lens ±1 mm in 50 µm steps.
Protocol
Collect triplicate sweeps: baseline, activator-in, activator-out (sham glass).
Record dark current; subtract. Keep LED current constant with driver feedback.
Analysis
Compare residuals to Malus fit; look for statistically significant structured deviations.
If effect localizes spatially, build a heatmap I(x,y) at the target θ.
Repeatability: day-to-day correlation of anomaly map ≥0.8 before claiming observation.
Controls
Swap order of optical elements to test for simple birefringence explanations.
Use a different photodiode and repeat.
Rotate the cracked element itself to test frame dependence.
If this plan works for you, I’ll expand the parts lists (with budget tiers) and produce ready-to-run worksheets (checklists + data sheets) next. Then we can circle back to the Philosophical & Cognitive Bridge using whatever the experiments actually show.

love it—let’s lock this in. here’s a clean first draft in the six sections you outlined, ready to run and easy to drop into a Methods section later.
Title: SAT ψ-Null / Visibility-Collapse Experiment — Methods Draft v0.1

1) Objective
Test for a “ψ-null” effect in which a cracked-lens + polarizer stack (the “activator”) produces a measurable, repeatable collapse or anomalous suppression of transmitted intensity (or fringe-like contrast) beyond classical expectations (Malus’ law + ordinary birefringence). The SAT-positive signature is a localized, orientation-specific intensity minimum that:
• exceeds the prediction of a calibrated classical model,
• follows (tracks) the cracked element when translated/rotated,
• disappears under sham substitutions or control reorderings,
• reproduces across days and minor apparatus changes.

2) Apparatus (off-the-shelf, ≈$300 bench)
Optical source & conditioning
• High-stability LED module (white or narrowband ~520–630 nm) with constant-current driver.
• 1–2 mm pinhole + short focal length lens (or LED collimator) for quasi-collimated beam.
• Optional diffuser (ground glass) to kill residual coherence/speckle.

Polarization & “activator”
• 2× linear polarizers, photographic or lab grade (extinction ratio ≥ 1:200 recommended).
• Cracked/strained glass element (“activator”): a cheap glass lens or slide, intentionally cracked under tape; mounted on XY micrometer stage (≥50 µm step).
• Optional: a quarter-wave plate for classical-birefringence control tests.

Detection & DAQ
• Silicon photodiode + transimpedance amplifier module (≥16-bit DAQ or USB DMM logging).
• Light baffle tube and black shroud (foam board or flocking) to suppress stray light.
• Rotation stage for analyzer polarizer (0.1° vernier; protractor + fine screw acceptable).
• Temperature probe near optics; stable PSU or UPS to reduce power-line fluctuations.

Mounting & safety
• 30–60 cm mini-optical rail or breadboard (plywood + rails acceptable).
• Micrometer XY stage for activator; kinematic mounts (or DIY clamps).
• Safety: crack glass under painter’s tape, wear eye protection, handle shards carefully.

Indicative cost split (very approximate)
• LED + driver $35–$60; polarizers $40–$80; photodiode+TIA $60–$120; DAQ $60–$120; mechanics/mounts $40–$100.

3) Alignment (geometry, distances, tolerances)
Nominal linear order (source → detector):
LED/Collimator → Polarizer P → (Activator on XY) → Analyzer A (rotatable) → Baffle → Photodiode

Distances (bench-friendly; tweak as needed)
• LED→P: 8–12 cm (set by collimation and uniform beam area)
• P→Activator: 5–10 cm (room to insert/remount)
• Activator→A: 3–6 cm (keeps beam footprint small across micro-translations)
• A→Detector: 8–12 cm (room for baffle tube)

Tolerances
• Beam height alignment: within 1 mm across the train.
• Tilt of components: ≤0.2° relative to beam (shim until the transmitted spot is centered).
• Analyzer angle setting: 0.1° resolution (near-crossed angles are critical).
• Spot size on detector: sized to remain fully within active area during XY raster.

Baseline checks
• Measure polarizer extinction: with no activator, record I(θ) and fit Malus to get extinction/background parameters.
• Verify LED current stability (±0.1%) and detector linearity (no saturation).

4) Protocol (step-by-step)
A. Baseline & calibration
1) Warm up electronics 15–30 min. Record ambient T.
2) With only P and A, sweep analyzer angle θ from 0→180° in 0.5° steps. Integrate 200–500 ms per point.
3) Fit Malus with non-ideal terms (see Analysis) to extract extinction, offset, and angular zero.
4) Insert SHAM glass (uncrazed) where the activator will sit; repeat θ sweep; store as control.

B. Activator runs
5) Replace SHAM with cracked element. Select three analyzer setpoints near the crossed region:
θ* ∈ { (θ_cross − 2°), (θ_cross − 1°), (θ_cross) }.
6) At each θ*, raster the activator XY over ±1.0 mm in 50–100 µm steps (grid ≥ 21×21). Acquire mean intensity per point (N=5–10 samples).
7) Repeat the raster with the cracked element rotated by 90° about the optical axis.
8) Repeat entire sequence with analyzer and polarizer swapped (P↔A order control).
9) Randomize condition order across blocks: BASE → SHAM → ACT (0°) → ACT (90°) → SHAM, etc.
10) Day-2 replication: rebuild alignment lightly (break/re-make mounts), re-run the sequence.

C. Optional modulation (improves SNR if available)
11) Modulate LED at ~200–500 Hz (small amplitude) and demodulate in software (lock-in) to reject ambient drift/flicker.

5) Measurements (what to record)
Per data point
• Condition (BASE/SHAM/ACT; ACT rotation 0°/90°; P↔A order)
• θ (analyzer angle, degrees), X, Y (µm), trial index
• Photodiode reading (mean of N samples), SD
• LED drive current/voltage, detector gain
• Ambient temperature, timestamp

Sampling & rates
• Angle sweeps: 0.5° steps; 200–500 ms integration; N=3–5 repeats per angle.
• Raster: 50–100 µm step; 5–10 samples/point; total map time per θ* ≈ 10–20 min.
• Dark frames: shutter/LED-off measurement every 2–3 minutes for offset subtraction.

Data hygiene
• Save raw and dark-subtracted values; never overwrite.
• Log apparatus changes inline (comment field).
• Version the calibration fit parameters for each session.

6) Analysis (separating SAT signal from classical optics)
Classical baseline model
• Use a non-ideal Malus form with leakage and offset:
I(θ) = I0 · [ (1−ε) · cos²(θ−θ0) + ε ] + B
where ε captures finite extinction/leakage, θ0 is mis-zero, and B is background.
• With a cracked element, extend model to allow slowly varying birefringent retardance ϕ(x,y):
I(θ, x, y) = I0 · F(θ, ϕ(x,y), ε, θ0) + B
(Fit ϕ(x,y) with a low-order smoothness prior; this captures classical, smooth spatial structure.)

SAT-positive signature (operational)
A) Localized intensity “collapse” pockets at θ* whose depth exceeds the fitted classical prediction by >k·σ (e.g., k=5) **and** survive multiple-comparisons control across the raster.
B) Tracking: the pocket’s (x,y) location co-moves with the activator during deliberate micro-translations.
C) Orientation dependence: pocket pattern changes when the cracked element is rotated by 90°, in a way not explained by the birefringence fit (i.e., residuals remain structured and significant).
D) Controls: pockets vanish (or reduce to classical residuals) with SHAM glass, with P↔A order swap if the hypothesis requires a specific ordering, and under day-2 rebuilds the effect recurs at correlated map locations (r ≥ 0.8).

Statistics & reporting
• For each map, compute residual R(θ*,x,y) = I_meas − I_classical_fit.
• Use permutation tests to assess cluster-level significance of negative residual regions (cluster-based thresholding).
• Report effect size (Cohen’s d) for the deepest pocket vs. local neighborhood; include 95% CI via bootstrap.
• Pre-register a minimal effect size of interest (e.g., |d|≥1.0 and cluster area ≥ a few pixels) to avoid p-hacking.
• Replicability: require same-sign residual clusters at θ* on ≥2 days with spatial correlation r≥0.8.

Controls & confounds (and mitigations)
• Speckle/interference (if any): prefer LED + diffuser; if using a laser, dither/diffuse and average.
• Polarizer stress birefringence: rotate/swizzle polarizers; swap their order; test a second set.
• Detector nonlinearity: verify response vs. neutral-density attenuation; stay in mid-range.
• Mechanical drift: re-center spot between rows; keep raster duration per map <20 min; use foam shroud.
• Ambient flicker: constant-current LED; optional lock-in demodulation.
• Background light: dark shroud; periodic dark frames; subtract B.
• Thermal shifts: log T; regress out slow drift; keep activator handling time consistent (no warm hands on glass).

Decision rule (Go/No-Go)
• GO if: at least one condition produces a residual cluster meeting the SAT-positive signature, reproduces on day-2 with r≥0.8, and fails under SHAM.
• NO-GO (revise) if: anomalies vanish under improved classical modeling (e.g., allowing spatially varying ϕ(x,y)), or do not replicate under rotation, swap, and day-2 rebuilds.

session_id, condition, theta_deg, x_um, y_um, trial, pd_mean, pd_sd, led_current_mA, detector_gain, temp_C, timestamp, notes

# Virtual bench: simulate SAT ψ-null experiment data and analysis
# - Generate sham and activator raster maps at a near-crossed analyzer angle
# - Include a localized "collapse pocket" only in the activator condition
# - Compute residuals (activator - sham), estimate significance, and detect the largest negative cluster
# - Produce figures (no specific colors set) and save a CSV dataset for download

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from math import pi

rng = np.random.default_rng(42)

# -----------------------------
# Helper functions
# -----------------------------
def cos2(x):
c = np.cos(x)
return c*c

def smooth2d(arr, ksize=7, iters=1):
"""Simple box blur without external deps."""
ker = np.ones((ksize, ksize), dtype=float) / (ksize*ksize)
out = arr.copy()
for _ in range(iters):
# pad with edge values
pad = ksize//2
padded = np.pad(out, ((pad,pad),(pad,pad)), mode='edge')
# convolution
tmp = np.zeros_like(out)
for i in range(out.shape[0]):
for j in range(out.shape[1]):
tmp[i,j] = np.sum(padded[i:i+ksize, j:j+ksize]*ker)
out = tmp
return out

def make_field(nx, ny, base=1.0, noise=0.02, ksize=9, iters=2):
f = base + rng.normal(0, noise, size=(ny, nx))
f = smooth2d(f, ksize=ksize, iters=iters)
return f

def gaussian2d(nx, ny, cx, cy, sx, sy, amp):
x = np.arange(nx)
y = np.arange(ny)
X, Y = np.meshgrid(x, y)
g = amp * np.exp(-(((X-cx)**2)/(2*sx*sx) + ((Y-cy)**2)/(2*sy*sy)))
return g

def find_largest_negative_cluster(mask):
"""4-neighbor connected components on boolean mask; return size and indices of largest component."""
ny, nx = mask.shape
visited = np.zeros_like(mask, dtype=bool)
best_size = 0
best_indices = []
for y in range(ny):
for x in range(nx):
if mask[y,x] and not visited[y,x]:
# BFS
q = [(y,x)]
visited[y,x] = True
comp = [(y,x)]
while q:
cy, cx = q.pop(0)
for dy, dx in [(-1,0),(1,0),(0,-1),(0,1)]:
nyy, nxx = cy+dy, cx+dx
if 0 <= nyy < ny and 0 <= nxx < nx and mask[nyy, nxx] and not visited[nyy, nxx]:
visited[nyy, nxx] = True
q.append((nyy, nxx))
comp.append((nyy, nxx))
if len(comp) > best_size:
best_size = len(comp)
best_indices = comp
return best_size, best_indices

# -----------------------------
# Simulation parameters
# -----------------------------
nx = 60 # columns (x)
ny = 60 # rows (y)
step_um = 50 # grid step (micrometers)
theta_cross = pi/2 # crossed nominal
theta_star = theta_cross # map near-crossed angle
I0 = 1.0
eps = 0.02 # polarizer leakage
theta0 = 0.03 # small mis-zero (rad)
B = 0.01 # background
read_noise = 0.002

# Smooth spatial amplitude and phase-like fields (classical)
amp_field = make_field(nx, ny, base=1.0, noise=0.08, ksize=11, iters=2) # amplitude
phase_field = make_field(nx, ny, base=0.0, noise=0.05, ksize=11, iters=2) # small phase shifts

# Sham map (classical only)
theta_eff = theta_star - theta0 + phase_field
I_sham = I0 * ((1-eps) * amp_field * cos2(theta_eff) + eps) + B
I_sham += rng.normal(0, read_noise, size=I_sham.shape)

# Activator map = sham + localized "collapse pocket" (negative deviation)
I_act = I_sham.copy()

# Define a collapse pocket (location random-ish, fixed seed)
cx, cy = 38, 22
sx, sy = 4.5, 3.5 # std dev in pixels
depth = 0.20 # 20% depth beyond classical
pocket = gaussian2d(nx, ny, cx, cy, sx, sy, amp=depth)

# Apply pocket: multiplicative collapse (clamped to a floor)
I_act = I_act * (1 - pocket)
I_act = np.clip(I_act, 0, None)
I_act += rng.normal(0, read_noise, size=I_act.shape)

# Residuals (activator - sham)
residual = I_act - I_sham

# Estimate noise sigma from sham differences via local MAD proxy
# Here we approximate sigma as the global std of sham minus a smoothed version (local structure removed)
sham_smooth = smooth2d(I_sham, ksize=9, iters=1)
resid_sham = I_sham - sham_smooth
sigma_est = np.std(resid_sham)

# Threshold for negative clusters (k-sigma rule)
k = 5.0
neg_mask = residual < (-k * sigma_est)
size, indices = find_largest_negative_cluster(neg_mask)

# Prepare CSV dataset
xs = np.arange(nx)*step_um
ys = np.arange(ny)*step_um
X, Y = np.meshgrid(xs, ys)

records = []
for cond, img in [("sham", I_sham), ("activator", I_act)]:
for j in range(ny):
for i in range(nx):
records.append({
"condition": cond,
"theta_deg": np.degrees(theta_star),
"x_um": int(X[j, i]),
"y_um": int(Y[j, i]),
"intensity": float(img[j, i])
})

df = pd.DataFrame(records)
csv_path = "/mnt/data/psi_null_sim_dataset.csv"
df.to_csv(csv_path, index=False)

# Save a simple angle sweep for reference (baseline Malus-like curve)
thetas = np.linspace(0, pi, 181)
I_curve = I0 * ((1-eps) * cos2(thetas - theta0) + eps) + B
curve_path = "/mnt/data/malus_curve.csv"
pd.DataFrame({"theta_deg": np.degrees(thetas), "intensity": I_curve}).to_csv(curve_path, index=False)

# -----------------------------
# Figures (each chart its own plot, no explicit colors)
# -----------------------------

# 1) Heatmap of residuals
plt.figure(figsize=(6,5))
plt.imshow(residual, origin='lower', extent=[xs.min(), xs.max(), ys.min(), ys.max()])
plt.title("Residuals: Activator - Sham (near-crossed θ*)")
plt.xlabel("x (µm)")
plt.ylabel("y (µm)")
plt.colorbar(label="Δ Intensity")
residual_fig_path = "/mnt/data/residual_heatmap.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(residual_fig_path, dpi=150)
plt.show()

# 2) Histogram of residuals
plt.figure(figsize=(6,4))
plt.hist(residual.ravel(), bins=60)
plt.title("Residual Distribution")
plt.xlabel("Δ Intensity")
plt.ylabel("Count")
hist_path = "/mnt/data/residual_histogram.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(hist_path, dpi=150)
plt.show()

# 3) Baseline Malus curve
plt.figure(figsize=(6,4))
plt.plot(np.degrees(thetas), I_curve)
plt.title("Non-ideal Malus Baseline (no activator)")
plt.xlabel("Analyzer angle θ (deg)")
plt.ylabel("Relative intensity")
malus_fig_path = "/mnt/data/malus_curve.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(malus_fig_path, dpi=150)
plt.show()

# Report cluster stats
cluster_depth = residual.min()
cluster_size_pixels = size
cluster_area_mm2 = cluster_size_pixels * (step_um*1e-3)**2

print("Estimated noise sigma (from sham):", float(sigma_est))
print("5-sigma threshold:", float(-k * sigma_est))
print("Minimum residual observed:", float(cluster_depth))
print("Largest negative cluster size (pixels):", cluster_size_pixels)
print("Largest negative cluster area (mm^2):", float(cluster_area_mm2))
print("CSV dataset saved to:", csv_path)
print("Malus curve CSV saved to:", curve_path)
print("Figures saved to:", residual_fig_path, hist_path, malus_fig_path)
STDOUT/STDERR

5-sigma threshold: -0.010019755983853709

Largest negative cluster area (mm^2): 0.0

Totally fine—thanks for the heads-up. Let’s keep it all on-paper/in-silico.
To make progress without a bench, I just built a virtual bench version of the ψ-null test so we can practice the analysis pipeline and hand someone else a ready-to-run workflow later. It simulates:

Thought for 35s
Let’s “run” a paper version first—ψ-modulated clock drift. Short, falsifiable, and easy to reason about.
Paper Lab Run — ψ-Modulated Clock Drift
Objective
Look for a path-specific frequency offset when an RF line is co-located with the “activator” stack.
Setup (thought-experiment constants)

Two 10 MHz refs (A,B), phase-compared at 1 Hz.
Identical cables; one RF path routed beside the activator (Path-X), the other kept clear (Path-Y).
Four conditions: Control, Activator@X, Activator@Y, Sham@X.
Each block T = 30 min.
What the numbers mean (detectability)
Phase drift from a fractional offset δ is: cycles = f·δ·T.
At f=10 MHz and T=1800 s: cycles ≈ 1.8×10¹⁰·δ.
If you can resolve ~0.05 cycles (~18°), your δ floor is ≈ 0.05 / 1.8×10¹⁰ ≈ 2.8×10⁻¹².
So any stable effect ≥3×10⁻¹² is clearly visible; 1×10⁻¹¹ is blatant.
Predictions
Condition SM (classical expectation) SAT (example)
Control δ≈0; slow symmetric drift only (thermal/ADEV) same
Activator@X δ≈0 (no optical→RF coupling) δ≈+1×10⁻¹¹ (path-specific)
Activator@Y δ≈0 δ≈−1×10⁻¹¹ (sign flips with path)
Sham@X δ≈0 δ≈0 (no effect)
Concrete outcomes (plugging numbers)
Take δ=±1×10⁻¹¹ as a minimally “surprising but plausible” SAT signal.
Then Δf = δ·f = 1e-11·1e7 = 1e-4 Hz;
cycles over 30 min = 1e-4·1800 = 0.18 cycles ≈ 65° phase drift.
Activator@X → phase ramps +65° over the block.
Activator@Y → ramps −65°.
Control/Sham → no consistent signed ramp after detrending.
Analysis plan (paper)
Compute phase difference ϕ(t); remove linear thermal drift using the Control block slope.
Estimate per-block mean frequency offset via Δϕ/T.
Paired test: (Activator@X − Sham@X) and (Activator@X − Activator@Y).
Path-swap check: sign reversal is mandatory under SAT; absent under SM.
Pre-register smallest effect of interest: |δ|≥3×10⁻¹².
Failure modes to beat (and how)
Cable delay asymmetry → swap cables; effect must follow the activator, not hardware.
EM pickup → Faraday sleeve + SDR sweep; Sham must null it.
Thermal gradient → bundle both lines; log temperature and regress.
Decision rule
Claim “SAT-positive” only if: (i) |δ|≥3×10⁻¹² with p<0.01, (ii) sign flips with path-swap, (iii) disappears with Sham, (iv) replicates on a fresh rebuild.
If this looks good, we can do a second paper run on ψ-null photometry next, then bridge the same “phase/visibility” logic to the filament-communication model.

# Virtual bench: ψ-null experiment — tightened analysis pipeline
#
# What this does:
# 1) Simulates sham & activator raster maps near crossed polarizers
# 2) Seeds a localized "collapse pocket" in the activator map
# 3) Computes residuals and detects largest negative cluster (4-neighbor)
# 4) Estimates noise via local smoothing residuals
# 5) Runs bootstrap CIs for pocket depth and cluster-mean residual
# 6) Runs a permutation test for condition difference within the pocket ROI
# 7) Saves CSVs and PNGs you can download

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from math import pi
from pathlib import Path
import json
from typing import Tuple, List

# -----------------------------
# Utility
# -----------------------------
rng = np.random.default_rng(1234)
OUT = Path("/mnt/data")
OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

def cos2(x):
c = np.cos(x)
return c*c

def smooth2d(arr, ksize=7, iters=1):
ker = np.ones((ksize, ksize), dtype=float) / (ksize*ksize)
out = arr.copy()
for _ in range(iters):
pad = ksize//2
padded = np.pad(out, ((pad,pad),(pad,pad)), mode='edge')
tmp = np.zeros_like(out)
# naive 2D convolution (kept explicit to avoid external deps)
for i in range(out.shape[0]):
for j in range(out.shape[1]):
tmp[i,j] = np.sum(padded[i:i+ksize, j:j+ksize]*ker)
out = tmp
return out

def make_field(nx, ny, base=1.0, noise=0.02, ksize=9, iters=1):
f = base + rng.normal(0, noise, size=(ny, nx))
f = smooth2d(f, ksize=ksize, iters=iters)
return f

def gaussian2d(nx, ny, cx, cy, sx, sy, amp):
x = np.arange(nx)
y = np.arange(ny)
X, Y = np.meshgrid(x, y)
g = amp * np.exp(-(((X-cx)**2)/(2*sx*sx) + ((Y-cy)**2)/(2*sy*sy)))
return g

def find_largest_negative_cluster(mask: np.ndarray) -> Tuple[int, List[Tuple[int,int]], np.ndarray]:
"""Return size, indices, and labeled component map for the largest 4-neighbor negative cluster."""
ny, nx = mask.shape
visited = np.zeros_like(mask, dtype=bool)
labels = np.zeros_like(mask, dtype=int)
best_size, best_indices, label_id = 0, [], 0
for y in range(ny):
for x in range(nx):
if mask[y,x] and not visited[y,x]:
label_id += 1
q = [(y,x)]
visited[y,x] = True
comp = [(y,x)]
labels[y,x] = label_id
while q:
cy, cx = q.pop(0)
for dy, dx in [(-1,0),(1,0),(0,-1),(0,1)]:
nyy, nxx = cy+dy, cx+dx
if 0 <= nyy < ny and 0 <= nxx < nx and mask[nyy, nxx] and not visited[nyy, nxx]:
visited[nyy, nxx] = True
q.append((nyy, nxx))
comp.append((nyy, nxx))
labels[nyy, nxx] = label_id
if len(comp) > best_size:
best_size = len(comp)
best_indices = comp
return best_size, best_indices, labels

# -----------------------------
# Simulation parameters
# -----------------------------
params = {
"nx": 60,
"ny": 60,
"step_um": 50,
"theta_cross_rad": float(pi/2),
"theta0_rad": 0.03, # analyzer mis-zero
"I0": 1.0,
"eps": 0.02, # polarizer leakage
"B": 0.01, # background
"read_noise": 0.002, # detector noise
# collapse pocket
"pocket_cx": 38,
"pocket_cy": 22,
"pocket_sx_px": 4.5,
"pocket_sy_px": 3.5,
"pocket_depth": 0.25 # 25% multiplicative dip (slightly stronger than before)
}
with open(OUT/"psi_null_sim_params.json", "w") as f:
json.dump(params, f, indent=2)

nx, ny = params["nx"], params["ny"]
step_um = params["step_um"]
theta_cross = params["theta_cross_rad"]
theta0 = params["theta0_rad"]
I0, eps, B = params["I0"], params["eps"], params["B"]
read_noise = params["read_noise"]

# Smooth spatial amplitude and phase-like fields
amp_field = make_field(nx, ny, base=1.0, noise=0.08, ksize=11, iters=2)
phase_field = make_field(nx, ny, base=0.0, noise=0.05, ksize=11, iters=2)

theta_star = theta_cross # near crossed
theta_eff = theta_star - theta0 + phase_field

# Sham (classical optics only)
I_sham = I0 * ((1-eps) * amp_field * cos2(theta_eff) + eps) + B
I_sham += rng.normal(0, read_noise, size=I_sham.shape)

# Activator = sham * (1 - pocket) + noise
pocket = gaussian2d(nx, ny, params["pocket_cx"], params["pocket_cy"],
params["pocket_sx_px"], params["pocket_sy_px"], amp=params["pocket_depth"])
I_act = I_sham * (1 - pocket)
I_act = np.clip(I_act, 0, None)
I_act += rng.normal(0, read_noise, size=I_act.shape)

# Residuals
residual = I_act - I_sham

# Estimate noise sigma from sham-vs-smoothed
sham_smooth = smooth2d(I_sham, ksize=9, iters=1)
resid_sham = I_sham - sham_smooth
sigma_est = float(np.std(resid_sham))

# Threshold & cluster detection
k_sigma = 5.0
neg_mask = residual < (-k_sigma * sigma_est)
size, indices, labels = find_largest_negative_cluster(neg_mask)

# Compute cluster stats if found
cluster_depth = float(residual.min())
cluster_mean = float(np.mean([residual[y, x] for (y, x) in indices])) if indices else np.nan
cluster_area_mm2 = size * (step_um*1e-3)**2

# -----------------------------
# Bootstrap & permutation testing
# -----------------------------
def bootstrap_ci(data, n_boot=5000, func=np.mean, ci=0.95):
data = np.asarray(data)
boots = []
for _ in range(n_boot):
sample = rng.choice(data, size=data.size, replace=True)
boots.append(func(sample))
boots = np.sort(boots)
lo = np.percentile(boots, (1-ci)/2*100)
hi = np.percentile(boots, (1+ci)/2*100)
return float(lo), float(hi)

# Define ROI as a 2-sigma ellipse around the seeded pocket center for analysis
yy, xx = np.meshgrid(np.arange(ny), np.arange(nx), indexing="ij")
roi = (((xx-params["pocket_cx"])**2)/(2*params["pocket_sx_px"]**2) +
((yy-params["pocket_cy"])**2)/(2*params["pocket_sy_px"]**2)) <= 1.0

# Extract ROI samples
activ_roi = I_act[roi].ravel()
sham_roi = I_sham[roi].ravel()
resid_roi = residual[roi].ravel()

# Bootstrap CI for mean residual in ROI
roi_mean = float(np.mean(resid_roi))
roi_ci = bootstrap_ci(resid_roi, n_boot=3000, func=np.mean, ci=0.95)

# Permutation test (two-sample) for activator < sham in ROI
def perm_test_less(a, b, n_perm=5000):
# test statistic: mean(a - b)
obs = np.mean(a - b)
comb = np.concatenate([a, b])
count = 0
for _ in range(n_perm):
rng.shuffle(comb)
a_s = comb[:a.size]
b_s = comb[a.size:]
stat = np.mean(a_s - b_s)
if stat <= obs: # one-sided: activator lower than sham
count += 1
p = (count + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
return float(obs), float(p)

perm_stat, perm_p = perm_test_less(activ_roi, sham_roi, n_perm=4000)

# -----------------------------
# Save datasets
# -----------------------------
xs = np.arange(nx)*step_um
ys = np.arange(ny)*step_um
X, Y = np.meshgrid(xs, ys)

records = []
for cond, img in [("sham", I_sham), ("activator", I_act)]:
for j in range(ny):
for i in range(nx):
records.append({
"condition": cond,
"theta_deg": np.degrees(theta_star),
"x_um": int(X[j, i]),
"y_um": int(Y[j, i]),
"intensity": float(img[j, i])
})
df = pd.DataFrame(records)
csv_path = OUT/"psi_null_sim_dataset.csv"
df.to_csv(csv_path, index=False)

# Baseline Malus curve
thetas = np.linspace(0, pi, 181)
I_curve = I0 * ((1-eps) * cos2(thetas - theta0) + eps) + B
curve_path = OUT/"malus_curve.csv"
pd.DataFrame({"theta_deg": np.degrees(thetas), "intensity": I_curve}).to_csv(curve_path, index=False)

# Save a preregistration outline
prereg = f"""ψ-Null Photometry (Virtual Bench) — Prereg Outline
Hypothesis
H1: The activator condition produces a localized negative residual (activator − sham < 0)
near crossed polarizers exceeding k·σ (k = {k_sigma}) and forming a connected cluster.

Design
Raster grid: {nx}×{ny} at {step_um} µm steps (near crossed θ*).
Conditions: sham vs activator; sham defines noise model via local-smoothing residuals.
Primary ROI: 2σ ellipse centered at seeded location (held constant across runs).

Primary Outcomes
1) Mean residual within ROI (expect < 0). One-sided permutation test (activator < sham).
2) Largest negative cluster size at threshold k·σ and its mean residual.
3) Cluster depth (min residual).

Decision Criteria
- Permutation p ≤ 0.01 (one-sided) AND
- Largest negative cluster size ≥ 20 pixels AND
- Bootstrap 95% CI of ROI mean residual excludes 0 (negative).

Controls
- Noise sigma estimated from sham–smooth residuals.
- Repeat with independent RNG seeds (simulated “days”).
- Sensitivity analysis over k ∈ [4, 6].

Notes
This prereg is for the simulation pipeline; a physical bench would include Malus baseline
verification, detector linearity checks, polarizer calibration, and element reordering.
"""
prereg_path = OUT/"psi_null_prereg.txt"
with open(prereg_path, "w") as f:
f.write(prereg)

# -----------------------------
# Figures (no explicit colors; one chart per figure)
# -----------------------------

plt.figure(figsize=(6,5))
plt.imshow(residual, origin='lower', extent=[xs.min(), xs.max(), ys.min(), ys.max()])
plt.title("Residuals: Activator - Sham (near-crossed θ*)")
plt.xlabel("x (µm)")
plt.ylabel("y (µm)")

plt.tight_layout()

plt.figure(figsize=(6,5))

plt.title(f"Negative Cluster Mask (< -{k_sigma:.1f}σ)")
plt.xlabel("x (µm)")
plt.ylabel("y (µm)")
cluster_mask_path = OUT/"negative_cluster_mask.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(cluster_mask_path, dpi=150)
plt.show()

# Residual histogram
plt.figure(figsize=(6,4))
plt.hist(residual.ravel(), bins=60)
plt.title("Residual Distribution")
plt.xlabel("Δ Intensity")
plt.ylabel("Count")
hist_path = OUT/"residual_histogram.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(hist_path, dpi=150)
plt.show()

# Malus baseline plot
plt.figure(figsize=(6,4))
plt.plot(np.degrees(thetas), I_curve)
plt.title("Non-ideal Malus Baseline (no activator)")
plt.xlabel("Analyzer angle θ (deg)")
plt.ylabel("Relative intensity")
malus_fig_path = OUT/"malus_curve.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(malus_fig_path, dpi=150)
plt.show()

summary = {
"sigma_est": sigma_est,
"k_sigma": k_sigma,
"threshold": -k_sigma*sigma_est,

}

# Virtual Bench: ψ-null repeatability harness across multiple "days"
#
# What this does:
# - Runs the tightened ψ-null simulation across N days (different RNG seeds)
# - Computes residual maps, 5σ cluster detection, ROI stats, permutation p-values
# - Computes spatial correlation in the ROI between Day 1 and each subsequent day
# - Saves per-day figures and a summary CSV + JSON
#
# Notes:
# - Uses matplotlib only, one chart per figure, no explicit colors set.
# - Outputs downloadable files in /mnt/data

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from math import pi
from pathlib import Path
import json

# -----------------------------
# Utilities
# -----------------------------
OUT = Path("/mnt/data/repeatability")
OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

def cos2(x):
c = np.cos(x)
return c*c

def smooth2d(arr, ksize=7, iters=1):
ker = np.ones((ksize, ksize), dtype=float) / (ksize*ksize)
out = arr.copy()
for _ in range(iters):
pad = ksize//2
padded = np.pad(out, ((pad,pad),(pad,pad)), mode='edge')
tmp = np.zeros_like(out)
for i in range(out.shape[0]):
for j in range(out.shape[1]):
tmp[i,j] = np.sum(padded[i:i+ksize, j:j+ksize]*ker)
out = tmp
return out

def make_field(rng, nx, ny, base=1.0, noise=0.02, ksize=9, iters=1):
f = base + rng.normal(0, noise, size=(ny, nx))
f = smooth2d(f, ksize=ksize, iters=iters)
return f

def gaussian2d(nx, ny, cx, cy, sx, sy, amp):
x = np.arange(nx)
y = np.arange(ny)
X, Y = np.meshgrid(x, y)
g = amp * np.exp(-(((X-cx)**2)/(2*sx*sx) + ((Y-cy)**2)/(2*sy*sy)))
return g

def find_largest_negative_cluster(mask):
ny, nx = mask.shape
visited = np.zeros_like(mask, dtype=bool)
best_size, best_indices = 0, []
for y in range(ny):
for x in range(nx):
if mask[y,x] and not visited[y,x]:
q = [(y,x)]
visited[y,x] = True
comp = [(y,x)]
while q:
cy, cx = q.pop(0)
for dy, dx in [(-1,0),(1,0),(0,-1),(0,1)]:
nyy, nxx = cy+dy, cx+dx
if 0 <= nyy < ny and 0 <= nxx < nx and mask[nyy, nxx] and not visited[nyy, nxx]:
visited[nyy, nxx] = True
q.append((nyy, nxx))
comp.append((nyy, nxx))
if len(comp) > best_size:
best_size = len(comp)
best_indices = comp
return best_size, best_indices

def perm_test_less(rng, a, b, n_perm=4000):
obs = np.mean(a - b)
comb = np.concatenate([a, b])
count = 0
for _ in range(n_perm):
rng.shuffle(comb)
a_s = comb[:a.size]
b_s = comb[a.size:]
stat = np.mean(a_s - b_s)
if stat <= obs:
count += 1
p = (count + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
return float(obs), float(p)

# -----------------------------
# Parameters
# -----------------------------
params = {
"N_days": 6,
"base_seed": 7000,
"nx": 60,
"ny": 60,
"step_um": 50,
"theta_cross_rad": float(pi/2),
"theta0_rad": 0.03,
"I0": 1.0,
"eps": 0.02,
"B": 0.01,
"read_noise": 0.002,
# collapse pocket nominal
"pocket_cx": 38,
"pocket_cy": 22,
"pocket_sx_px": 4.5,
"pocket_sy_px": 3.5,
"pocket_depth": 0.25,
# day-to-day jitter of activator (simulated rebuild)
"jitter_px_sd": 1.0,
# detection threshold in sigmas
"k_sigma": 5.0
}

with open(OUT/"repeatability_params.json", "w") as f:
json.dump(params, f, indent=2)

nx, ny = params["nx"], params["ny"]
theta_star = params["theta_cross_rad"]
theta0 = params["theta0_rad"]
I0, eps, B = params["I0"], params["eps"], params["B"]
read_noise = params["read_noise"]
jitter_sd = params["jitter_px_sd"]
k_sigma = params["k_sigma"]

# ROI (defined around nominal pocket center with 2σ ellipse)
yy, xx = np.meshgrid(np.arange(ny), np.arange(nx), indexing="ij")
roi = (((xx-params["pocket_cx"])**2)/(2*params["pocket_sx_px"]**2) +
((yy-params["pocket_cy"])**2)/(2*params["pocket_sy_px"]**2)) <= 1.0

# -----------------------------
# Run per-day sims
# -----------------------------
summ_rows = []
resid_maps = [] # store residuals for ROI-correlation

for day in range(1, params["N_days"] + 1):
seed = params["base_seed"] + day
rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

# fields
amp_field = make_field(rng, nx, ny, base=1.0, noise=0.08, ksize=11, iters=2)
phase_field = make_field(rng, nx, ny, base=0.0, noise=0.05, ksize=11, iters=2)

theta_eff = theta_star - theta0 + phase_field

# sham
I_sham = I0 * ((1-eps) * amp_field * cos2(theta_eff) + eps) + B
I_sham += rng.normal(0, read_noise, size=I_sham.shape)

# activator with day-to-day pocket jitter
cx = params["pocket_cx"] + rng.normal(0, jitter_sd)
cy = params["pocket_cy"] + rng.normal(0, jitter_sd)
pocket = gaussian2d(nx, ny, cx, cy, params["pocket_sx_px"], params["pocket_sy_px"], amp=params["pocket_depth"])
I_act = I_sham * (1 - pocket)
I_act = np.clip(I_act, 0, None)
I_act += rng.normal(0, read_noise, size=I_act.shape)

residual = I_act - I_sham
resid_maps.append(residual.copy())

# Noise sigma from sham-smooth
sham_smooth = smooth2d(I_sham, ksize=9, iters=1)
resid_sham = I_sham - sham_smooth
sigma_est = float(np.std(resid_sham))

# Cluster detection at kσ
neg_mask = residual < (-k_sigma * sigma_est)
size, indices = find_largest_negative_cluster(neg_mask)

roi_vals = residual[roi].ravel()
roi_mean = float(np.mean(roi_vals))

# One-sided permutation test (activator < sham) within ROI
activ_roi = I_act[roi].ravel()
sham_roi = I_sham[roi].ravel()
perm_stat, perm_p = perm_test_less(rng, activ_roi, sham_roi, n_perm=3000)

# Save per-day figures
fig1 = plt.figure(figsize=(6,5))
plt.imshow(residual, origin='lower')
plt.title(f"Day {day}: Residuals (Activator - Sham)")
plt.xlabel("x (px)")
plt.ylabel("y (px)")
cb = plt.colorbar()
cb.set_label("Δ Intensity")
path_resid = OUT/f"day{day:02d}_residuals.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(path_resid, dpi=150)
plt.show()

fig2 = plt.figure(figsize=(6,5))
plt.imshow(neg_mask, origin='lower')
plt.title(f"Day {day}: Negative Cluster Mask (< -{k_sigma:.1f}σ)")
plt.xlabel("x (px)")
plt.ylabel("y (px)")
path_mask = OUT/f"day{day:02d}_cluster_mask.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(path_mask, dpi=150)
plt.show()

summ_rows.append({
"day": day,
"seed": seed,
"sigma_est": sigma_est,
"threshold": -k_sigma * sigma_est,
"min_residual": float(residual.min()),
"largest_cluster_size_px": int(size),
"roi_mean_residual": roi_mean,
"perm_stat_mean_a_minus_b": float(perm_stat),
"perm_p_one_sided": float(perm_p),
"residual_png": str(path_resid),
"cluster_mask_png": str(path_mask)
})

a = a.ravel()

denom = (np.sqrt((am*am).sum()) * np.sqrt((bm*bm).sum()))

# Virtual Bench: ψ-null repeatability harness across multiple "days"
#
# What this does (re-run after session reset):
# - Runs tightened ψ-null simulation across N days (different RNG seeds)
# - Computes residual maps, 5σ cluster detection, ROI stats, permutation p-values
# - Computes spatial correlation in the ROI between Day 1 and each subsequent day
# - Saves per-day figures and a summary CSV + JSON
#
# Notes:
# - matplotlib only, one chart per figure, no explicit colors set.
# - Outputs downloadable files in /mnt/data/repeatability

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from math import pi
from pathlib import Path
import json

# -----------------------------
# Utilities
# -----------------------------
OUT = Path("/mnt/data/repeatability")
OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

def cos2(x):
c = np.cos(x)
return c*c

def smooth2d(arr, ksize=7, iters=1):
ker = np.ones((ksize, ksize), dtype=float) / (ksize*ksize)
out = arr.copy()
for _ in range(iters):
pad = ksize//2
padded = np.pad(out, ((pad,pad),(pad,pad)), mode='edge')
tmp = np.zeros_like(out)
for i in range(out.shape[0]):
for j in range(out.shape[1]):
tmp[i,j] = np.sum(padded[i:i+ksize, j:j+ksize]*ker)
out = tmp
return out

def make_field(rng, nx, ny, base=1.0, noise=0.02, ksize=9, iters=1):
f = base + rng.normal(0, noise, size=(ny, nx))
f = smooth2d(f, ksize=ksize, iters=iters)
return f

def gaussian2d(nx, ny, cx, cy, sx, sy, amp):
x = np.arange(nx)
y = np.arange(ny)
X, Y = np.meshgrid(x, y)
g = amp * np.exp(-(((X-cx)**2)/(2*sx*sx) + ((Y-cy)**2)/(2*sy*sy)))
return g

def find_largest_negative_cluster(mask):
ny, nx = mask.shape
visited = np.zeros_like(mask, dtype=bool)
best_size, best_indices = 0, []
for y in range(ny):
for x in range(nx):
if mask[y,x] and not visited[y,x]:
q = [(y,x)]
visited[y,x] = True
comp = [(y,x)]
while q:
cy, cx = q.pop(0)
for dy, dx in [(-1,0),(1,0),(0,-1),(0,1)]:
nyy, nxx = cy+dy, cx+dx
if 0 <= nyy < ny and 0 <= nxx < nx and mask[nyy, nxx] and not visited[nyy, nxx]:
visited[nyy, nxx] = True
q.append((nyy, nxx))
comp.append((nyy, nxx))
if len(comp) > best_size:
best_size = len(comp)
best_indices = comp
return best_size, best_indices

def perm_test_less(rng, a, b, n_perm=4000):
obs = np.mean(a - b)
comb = np.concatenate([a, b])
count = 0
for _ in range(n_perm):
rng.shuffle(comb)
a_s = comb[:a.size]
b_s = comb[a.size:]
stat = np.mean(a_s - b_s)
if stat <= obs:
count += 1
p = (count + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
return float(obs), float(p)

def pearson_r(a, b):
a = a.ravel().astype(float)
b = b.ravel().astype(float)
am = a - a.mean()
bm = b - b.mean()
denom = (np.sqrt((am*am).sum()) * np.sqrt((bm*bm).sum()))
if denom == 0:
return float("nan")
return float((am*bm).sum() / denom)

# -----------------------------
# Parameters
# -----------------------------
params = {
"N_days": 6,
"base_seed": 7000,
"nx": 60,
"ny": 60,
"step_um": 50,
"theta_cross_rad": float(pi/2),
"theta0_rad": 0.03,
"I0": 1.0,
"eps": 0.02,
"B": 0.01,
"read_noise": 0.002,
# collapse pocket nominal
"pocket_cx": 38,
"pocket_cy": 22,
"pocket_sx_px": 4.5,
"pocket_sy_px": 3.5,
"pocket_depth": 0.25,
# day-to-day jitter of activator (simulated rebuild)
"jitter_px_sd": 1.0,
# detection threshold in sigmas
"k_sigma": 5.0
}

with open(OUT/"repeatability_params.json", "w") as f:
json.dump(params, f, indent=2)

nx, ny = params["nx"], params["ny"]
theta_star = params["theta_cross_rad"]
theta0 = params["theta0_rad"]
I0, eps, B = params["I0"], params["eps"], params["B"]
read_noise = params["read_noise"]
jitter_sd = params["jitter_px_sd"]
k_sigma = params["k_sigma"]

# ROI (defined around nominal pocket center with 2σ ellipse)
yy, xx = np.meshgrid(np.arange(ny), np.arange(nx), indexing="ij")
roi = (((xx-params["pocket_cx"])**2)/(2*params["pocket_sx_px"]**2) +
((yy-params["pocket_cy"])**2)/(2*params["pocket_sy_px"]**2)) <= 1.0

# -----------------------------
summ_rows = []

for day in range(1, params["N_days"] + 1):

# fields

phase_field = make_field(rng, nx, ny, base=0.0, noise=0.05, ksize=11, iters=2)

# activator with day-to-day pocket jitter
cx = params["pocket_cx"] + rng.normal(0, jitter_sd)
cy = params["pocket_cy"] + rng.normal(0, jitter_sd)
pocket = gaussian2d(nx, ny, cx, cy, params["pocket_sx_px"], params["pocket_sy_px"], amp=params["pocket_depth"])
I_act = I_sham * (1 - pocket)
I_act = np.clip(I_act, 0, None)
I_act += rng.normal(0, read_noise, size=I_act.shape)

resid_sham = I_sham - sham_smooth

roi_vals = residual[roi].ravel()
roi_mean = float(np.mean(roi_vals))

# One-sided permutation test (activator < sham) within ROI
activ_roi = I_act[roi].ravel()
sham_roi = I_sham[roi].ravel()
perm_stat, perm_p = perm_test_less(np.random.default_rng(seed+999), activ_roi, sham_roi, n_perm=3000)

# Save per-day figures
plt.figure(figsize=(6,5))
plt.imshow(residual, origin='lower')
plt.title(f"Day {day}: Residuals (Activator - Sham)")

plt.show()

plt.imshow(neg_mask, origin='lower')
plt.title(f"Day {day}: Negative Cluster Mask (< -{k_sigma:.1f}σ)")
plt.xlabel("x (px)")
plt.ylabel("y (px)")
path_mask = OUT/f"day{day:02d}_cluster_mask.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(path_mask, dpi=150)
plt.show()

summ_rows.append({
"day": day,
"seed": seed,
"sigma_est": sigma_est,
"threshold": -k_sigma * sigma_est,
"min_residual": float(residual.min()),
"largest_cluster_size_px": int(size),
"roi_mean_residual": roi_mean,
"perm_stat_mean_a_minus_b": float(perm_stat),
"perm_p_one_sided": float(perm_p),

Ψ-Null Repeatability Summary (Days 1–6)


perfect—staying in-silico works. you said “always show details,” so here’s a full, auditable repeatability pass on the ψ-null virtual bench, plus downloads.
What I ran
Simulated 6 “days” (fresh seeds), each with sham vs activator maps near crossed polarizers.
Kept the same pocket physics but added day-to-day ±1 px jitter in the activator’s center (to mimic a light rebuild).
For each day: estimated noise σ from sham (sham–smooth residuals), thresholded residuals at −5σ, found the largest negative cluster, computed ROI mean residual, and ran a one-sided permutation test (activator < sham) inside the ROI.
Grab the files

(# you can also open the directory: sandbox:/mnt/data/repeatability)
Key results (per the summary you can open above)
Noise σ per day ≈ 0.00195–0.00201 → 5σ thresholds ≈ −0.00973 to −0.01007.
Min residuals reach about −0.0094 to −0.0117; some days cross −5σ.
Permutation tests (ROI, one-sided) are consistently tiny (p ≈ 3.3×10⁻⁴), as expected from a seeded pocket.
Largest −5σ cluster size is small (0–2 px) at this threshold—by design, we’re testing the edge case.
ROI correlation vs Day-1 drops with jitter (r ≈ 0.32, 0.28, 0.00, 0.19, 0.04 for Days 2–6). That’s the rebuild effect: the ROI is fixed in bench coordinates while the pocket actually shifts a bit.
What this tells us
The pipeline is sensitive (p-values and ROI means fire), but strict −5σ clusters are tiny unless the pocket is deeper or the threshold is relaxed. That’s good for exercising “GO/NO-GO” logic rather than inflating wins.
Correlation across days is not a fair test without compensating for recenter/realign — small spatial jitter tanks r even when the pocket is real.
Two quick upgrades I can add (say the word)
A) Recentered day-to-day correlation (recommended)
Instead of correlating raw ROI pixels across days:
Find the negative-residual centroid (or the argmin) inside a broad search window.
Extract a fixed-size patch (e.g., 15×15 px) centered on that point.
Correlate patches to Day-1’s patch.
This typically drives r→0.8–0.95 in a true, stable effect with only translational jitter.
B) Sensitivity sweep & power sketch
Sweep pocket depth ∈ {0.10, 0.15, 0.20, 0.25}, thresholds k ∈ {4, 4.5, 5, 5.5, 6}.
Report hit-rate for: (i) ROI perm-test p≤0.01, (ii) any −kσ cluster ≥20 px, (iii) recentered r≥0.8.
This gives you a pre-registered decision surface.

# Paper Run: ψ-Modulated Clock Drift — synthetic dataset + robust analysis
#
# What this notebook does:
# 1) Simulates five 30-min blocks (1 Hz) at 10 MHz: Control (A), Activator@X (B), Sham@X (C),
# Activator@Y (D), Control after cable swap (A′). Randomizes block order and hides labels.
# 2) Generates phase vs. time with occasional glitches, thermal drift, and measurement noise.
# 3) Unwraps phase and estimates slopes using a robust Huber IRLS regression per block and per 5-min subwindow.
# 4) Converts slopes → fractional frequency offsets δ_hat = slope/(2π f).
# 5) Detrends by subtracting the average Control slope (A and A′).
# 6) Computes pre-declared contrasts: C1 (X−Sham), C2 (X−Y), C3 ((X+Y)/2 − Control).
# 7) Runs paired permutation tests on windowed estimates; reports p-values.
# 8) Saves CSVs for time series and a summary table; plots unwrapped phase with robust fit and window histograms.
#
# Notes: Uses matplotlib only. One plot per figure. No explicit colors set. Internet is disabled.

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from pathlib import Path
from math import pi
import json
from hashlib import sha256

# -----------------------------
# Configuration
# -----------------------------
OUT = Path("/mnt/data/clock_paper_run")
OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

fs = 1.0 # Hz sampling
T_block = 30*60 # 30 minutes in seconds
f0 = 10_000_000.0 # 10 MHz
n_blocks = 5
win_len = 5*60 # 5 minutes windows
rng = np.random.default_rng(20250811)

# True fractional offsets per condition (before detrend)
true_deltas = {
"A_Control": 1.0e-12, # small baseline
"B_ActivX": 1.0e-11, # + sign
"C_ShamX": 0.0e-12, # ~0
"D_ActivY": -1.0e-11, # − sign (sign flip check)
"A2_Control": 0.8e-12 # second control (cable swap)
}

# Thermal/common drift term added to all blocks (cancels under detrend but tests estimator)
thermal_drift = 0.5e-12

# Measurement noise parameters
phase_noise_sd = 0.15 # radians of measurement noise
rw_phase_sd = 0.003 # per-step phase random walk (slow wander)
n_glitches = 3 # outlier spikes per block (phase jumps), robust fit should resist

# -----------------------------
# Helper functions
# -----------------------------
def simulate_phase_block(delta_frac, seed):
"""Simulate wrapped and unwrapped phase for one 30-min block at 1 Hz."""
local_rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
t = np.arange(T_block) / fs

# True slope in rad/s
slope = 2*np.pi * f0 * (delta_frac + thermal_drift)

# Build phase: linear + random walk + occasional glitches
phi = slope * t
rw = np.cumsum(local_rng.normal(0, rw_phase_sd, size=t.size))
phi = phi + rw

# Insert glitches (single-sample big jumps)
glitch_idx = local_rng.choice(t.size, size=n_glitches, replace=False)
for g in glitch_idx:
phi[g] += local_rng.normal(8*np.pi, 3*np.pi) # big wraps

# Measurement noise and wrapping to [0, 2π)
meas = local_rng.normal(0, phase_noise_sd, size=t.size)
phi_wrapped = (phi + meas) % (2*np.pi)

# Unwrap as an instrument would
phi_unwrapped = np.unwrap(phi_wrapped)

return t, phi_wrapped, phi_unwrapped

def huber_irls_slope(t, y, k=1.5, iters=10):
"""Robust slope via Huber IRLS: y ≈ a + b t"""
t = t - t.mean()
X = np.vstack([np.ones_like(t), t]).T
w = np.ones_like(y)
for _ in range(iters):
# Weighted least squares
W = np.diag(w)
beta = np.linalg.pinv(X.T @ W @ X) @ (X.T @ W @ y)
yhat = X @ beta
r = y - yhat
# Robust scale via MAD
mad = np.median(np.abs(r - np.median(r))) + 1e-12
sigma = 1.4826 * mad
# Huber weights
z = r / (sigma if sigma > 0 else 1.0)
w = np.where(np.abs(z) <= k, 1.0, k/np.abs(z))
return float(beta[1]), float(beta[0])

def window_bounds(n, win_len):
starts = np.arange(0, n, win_len)
bounds = [(s, min(s+win_len, n)) for s in starts]
# Ensure exact 6 windows of equal length if possible
bounds = bounds[:n//win_len]
return bounds

def paired_perm_pvalue(diffs, n_perm=10000, alternative="greater", rng=None):
"""
Paired permutation test by random sign flips on diffs.
H0: mean(diffs) == 0. H1: mean(diffs) > 0 (or < 0).
"""
if rng is None:
rng = np.random.default_rng()
diffs = np.asarray(diffs, dtype=float)
obs = diffs.mean()
count = 0
for _ in range(n_perm):
signs = rng.choice([-1, 1], size=diffs.size)
stat = (diffs * signs).mean()
if alternative == "greater":
count += (stat >= obs)
elif alternative == "less":
count += (stat <= obs)
else:
count += (abs(stat) >= abs(obs))
p = (count + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
return float(obs), float(p)

# -----------------------------
# Build randomized block order and blind labels
# -----------------------------
block_order = list(true_deltas.keys())
rng.shuffle(block_order)

# Blind mapping (hash labels to tokens)
blind_map = {}
for name in block_order:
token = sha256(name.encode()).hexdigest()[:8].upper()
blind_map[token] = name

# -----------------------------
# Simulate all blocks
# -----------------------------
records = []
timeseries_paths = []
window_rows = []

for name in block_order:
delta = true_deltas[name]
t, phi_wrapped, phi_unwrapped = simulate_phase_block(delta, seed=hash(name) % (2**32))
# Robust slope for full block
slope, intercept = huber_irls_slope(t, phi_unwrapped)
delta_hat = slope / (2*np.pi * f0)

# Per-window slopes (5-min windows)
bounds = window_bounds(len(t), win_len)
win_deltas = []
for i, (s, e) in enumerate(bounds):
s_t = t[s:e]
s_phi = phi_unwrapped[s:e]
w_slope, _ = huber_irls_slope(s_t, s_phi)
win_deltas.append(w_slope / (2*np.pi * f0))
window_rows.append({
"block_name_blind": [k for k, v in blind_map.items() if v == name][0],
"window_ix": i+1,
"delta_hat": w_slope / (2*np.pi * f0)
})

# Save timeseries CSV
df_ts = pd.DataFrame({
"t_s": t,
"phi_wrapped_rad": phi_wrapped,
"phi_unwrapped_rad": phi_unwrapped
})
ts_path = OUT / f"{name}_timeseries.csv"
df_ts.to_csv(ts_path, index=False)
timeseries_paths.append((name, str(ts_path)))

records.append({
"block_name_blind": [k for k, v in blind_map.items() if v == name][0],
"true_delta": delta,
"slope_rad_per_s": slope,
"delta_hat": delta_hat,
"n_windows": len(win_deltas),
"windows_mean_delta_hat": float(np.mean(win_deltas)),
"windows_std_delta_hat": float(np.std(win_deltas, ddof=1))
})

# Plot: unwrapped phase with robust fit
plt.figure(figsize=(8,4))
plt.plot(t, phi_unwrapped)
plt.title(f"{name}: Phase (unwrapped)")
plt.xlabel("Time (s)")
plt.ylabel("Phase (rad)")
# overlay robust fit line
plt.plot(t, intercept + slope*t)
fig_path = OUT / f"{name}_phase_fit.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(fig_path, dpi=150)
plt.show()

# Save the blind mapping for later unblinding
with open(OUT/"blind_map.json", "w") as f:
json.dump(blind_map, f, indent=2)

# Block summary (still blinded)
df_blocks = pd.DataFrame(records)
df_blocks.to_csv(OUT/"block_summary_blinded.csv", index=False)

# -----------------------------
# DETREND using Controls (A and A2): average of their windowed means
# -----------------------------
# Unblind to identify controls for detrend only (estimator is already frozen)
control_tokens = [k for k,v in blind_map.items() if v in ["A_Control", "A2_Control"]]

# Build windowed dataframe for contrasts
df_windows = pd.DataFrame(window_rows)

# Compute control baseline from the two control blocks (window means)
ctrl_mean = df_windows[df_windows["block_name_blind"].isin(control_tokens)].groupby("block_name_blind")["delta_hat"].mean().mean()

# Detrend all window deltas
df_windows["delta_hat_detrended"] = df_windows["delta_hat"] - ctrl_mean

# For reporting, compute per-block stats after detrend
block_stats = df_windows.groupby("block_name_blind")["delta_hat_detrended"].agg(["mean","std","count"]).reset_index()
block_stats.rename(columns={"mean":"delta_mean_detrended","std":"delta_sd_detrended","count":"n_windows"}, inplace=True)

# Map tokens to human names for the final (unblinded) table
block_stats["block_name"] = block_stats["block_name_blind"].map(blind_map)
cols = ["block_name","block_name_blind","n_windows","delta_mean_detrended","delta_sd_detrended"]
final_stats = block_stats[cols].sort_values("block_name")
final_stats.to_csv(OUT/"block_stats_unblinded.csv", index=False)

# -----------------------------
# CONTRASTS (C1, C2, C3) on windowed estimates
# -----------------------------
# Extract windows per condition
def pick(name):
token = [k for k,v in blind_map.items() if v == name][0]
return df_windows[df_windows["block_name_blind"] == token]["delta_hat_detrended"].to_numpy()

X = pick("B_ActivX")
Y = pick("D_ActivY")
S = pick("C_ShamX")
A = np.concatenate([pick("A_Control"), pick("A2_Control")]) # combine controls

# C1: X − Sham (one-sided, greater)
n_pair = min(len(X), len(S))
C1_diffs = (X[:n_pair] - S[:n_pair])
C1_stat, C1_p = paired_perm_pvalue(C1_diffs, n_perm=20000, alternative="greater", rng=rng)

# C2: X − Y (one-sided, greater) [expect positive because X>0, Y<0]
n_pair2 = min(len(X), len(Y))
C2_diffs = (X[:n_pair2] - Y[:n_pair2])
C2_stat, C2_p = paired_perm_pvalue(C2_diffs, n_perm=20000, alternative="greater", rng=rng)

# C3: (X + Y)/2 − Control (two-sided, expect ~0)
# build control pairs same size as average
A_mean = A.mean()
C3_vals = ((X[:n_pair2] + Y[:n_pair2]) / 2.0) - A_mean
# Two-sided permutation on centered values
C3_stat, C3_p = paired_perm_pvalue(C3_vals - C3_vals.mean(), n_perm=20000, alternative="two-sided", rng=rng)

# Decision checks
def within(x, band):
return (np.abs(x) <= band)

delta_X = X.mean()
delta_Y = Y.mean()
delta_S = S.mean()

decision = {
"thresh_abs": 3e-12,
"sham_band": 1e-12,
"abs_X_ok": abs(delta_X) >= 3e-12,
"abs_Y_ok": abs(delta_Y) >= 3e-12,
"opposite_signs": np.sign(delta_X) == -np.sign(delta_Y),
"sham_ok": within(delta_S, 1e-12),
"C1_p_ok": C1_p < 0.01,
"C2_p_ok": C2_p < 0.01,
"replicate_placeholder": True # single session; in real use require second session pass
}

# -----------------------------
# Plots: window histograms per block
# -----------------------------
for name in ["A_Control","B_ActivX","C_ShamX","D_ActivY","A2_Control"]:
token = [k for k,v in blind_map.items() if v == name][0]
vals = df_windows[df_windows["block_name_blind"] == token]["delta_hat_detrended"].to_numpy()
plt.figure(figsize=(6,4))
plt.hist(vals, bins=10)
plt.title(f"{name}: Window δ (detrended)")
plt.xlabel("δ (fractional)")
plt.ylabel("Count")
figp = OUT / f"{name}_window_delta_hist.png"
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(figp, dpi=150)
plt.show()

# -----------------------------
# Save report
# -----------------------------
report = {
"randomized_block_order_blinded": block_order,
"blind_tokens": blind_map,
"controls_used_for_baseline": [k for k,v in blind_map.items() if v in ["A_Control","A2_Control"]],
"control_baseline_mean_delta": float(ctrl_mean),
"per_block_stats": final_stats.to_dict(orient="records"),
"contrasts": {
"C1_X_minus_Sham": {"mean_diff": float(C1_stat), "p_value_one_sided_greater": float(C1_p)},
"C2_X_minus_Y": {"mean_diff": float(C2_stat), "p_value_one_sided_greater": float(C2_p)},
"C3_avgXY_minus_Control": {"mean_diff": float(C3_stat), "p_value_two_sided": float(C3_p)},
},
"decision_checks": decision,
"paths": {
"timeseries_csvs": [p for _, p in timeseries_paths],
"block_summary_blinded_csv": str(OUT/"block_summary_blinded.csv"),
"block_stats_unblinded_csv": str(OUT/"block_stats_unblinded.csv"),
"blind_map_json": str(OUT/"blind_map.json"),
"report_json": str(OUT/"clock_paper_report.json")
}
}
with open(OUT/"clock_paper_report.json", "w") as f:
json.dump(report, f, indent=2)

# Show compact textual summary
print("Detrended per-block means (δ, fractional):")
print(final_stats[["block_name","delta_mean_detrended"]].to_string(index=False))
print("\nContrasts (windowed):")
print(f"C1 (X−Sham) mean diff: {C1_stat:.3e}, p(one-sided, >0)={C1_p:.3g}")
print(f"C2 (X−Y) mean diff: {C2_stat:.3e}, p(one-sided, >0)={C2_p:.3g}")
print(f"C3 ((X+Y)/2−Control): {C3_stat:.3e}, p(two-sided)={C3_p:.3g}")
print("\nDecision checks:", json.dumps(decision, indent=2))

print("\nDownloads:")

print(" - Blind map:", str(OUT/'blind_map.json'))

Thought for 18s
Love those recentered r’s — that’s exactly the fairness fix we needed. Let’s lock the protocol while the picture’s clean. Here’s a print-ready one-pager you can hand to anyone (or drop into Methods). I included the recentered-correlation criterion you suggested.
Title: ψ-Modulated Clock Drift — Paper-Run Checklist (v1.0)

Objective
Detect a path-specific fractional frequency offset δ when an RF path is co-located with the “activator,” controlling for thermal/common drift and channel asymmetry.

Bench Constants (for paper runs)
Carrier f0 = 10 MHz; sample rate = 1 Hz; block length = 30 min; windows = 6 × 5 min.

Block Plan (randomize order; blind labels)
[A] Control [B] Activator@X [C] Sham@X [D] Activator@Y [A′] Control (after cable swap)
☐ Randomize order (A,B,C,D,A′) ☐ Hash labels for blinding ☐ Start temp & supply logging

Acquisition (each 30-min block)
☐ Record phase ϕ_wrapped(t) (radians) at 1 Hz
☐ Unwrap: ϕ(t) = unwrap(ϕ_wrapped(t))
☐ Log: temperature, supply voltage, notes, exact block label (kept blinded to analyst)

Estimator (frozen before unblinding)
Definition: δ̂_block = slope(ϕ(t)) / (2π f0), where slope is robust.
☐ Robust linear fit (Huber IRLS, k≈1.5) on the full block → δ̂_block
☐ Windowed fits: 6 windows × 5 min → {δ̂_win}
☐ Save slopes + residuals; flag any fit with R² < 0.98 for review (glitches tolerated)

Session Detrend & Asymmetry Control
☐ Compute baseline = mean({δ̂_win} from A and A′) → δ̂′ = δ̂ − baseline
☐ Verify A and A′ agree within 1×10⁻¹² (window means); else note “asymmetry” and rerun

Pre-declared Contrasts (windowed, detrended values)
C1: Activator@X − Sham@X → path-local effect?
C2: Activator@X − Activator@Y → sign-flip check (must be opposite sign)
C3: (Activator@X + Activator@Y)/2 − Control → should ≈ 0 (global effect check)
Stats: paired permutation (sign-flip) over windows
☐ C1 one-sided ( > 0 ) ☐ C2 one-sided ( > 0 ) ☐ C3 two-sided

Decision Rule (all required; prereg SOI = 3×10⁻¹²)
☐ |mean(δ̂′_X)| ≥ 3×10⁻¹² AND |mean(δ̂′_Y)| ≥ 3×10⁻¹²
☐ sign(δ̂′_X) = − sign(δ̂′_Y)
☐ Sham within ±1×10⁻¹² of Control window mean band
☐ Permutation p < 0.01 for C1 and C2; C3 non-sig (|p−0.5| small)
☐ Replicates on fresh rebuild (new seating / re-mounted activator)

Repeatability (recentered correlation — required for “stable feature” claim)
Search window: ±8 px; patch = 15×15 (optionally 21×21); pre-smooth σ=1 px (optional)
Procedure:
1) For each day, find the most-negative residual pixel in the search window.
2) Extract centered patch; lightly smooth (optional).
3) Compute Pearson r with Day-1’s patch.
Criterion:
☐ r ≥ 0.80 after recentering (and smoothing if pre-declared) → “stable feature”
☐ 0.3 ≤ r < 0.8 → “edge/noisy feature” (report but do not claim stability)
☐ r < 0.3 → not repeatable

Failure-Mode Kill Switches (invalidate if triggered)
☐ EM pickup: Sham reproduces Activator effect or SDR not quiet → invalidate session
☐ Thermal skew: effect tracks cable identity, not activator side → invalidate; must follow activator on swap
☐ Analysis bias: any estimator changes after unblinding → invalidate (rerun blinded)

File Conventions
timeseries_{BLOCK}.csv: t_s, phi_wrapped_rad, phi_unwrapped_rad
windows_{BLOCK}.csv: window_ix, delta_hat, delta_hat_detrended
session_summary.csv: block_name, n_windows, mean_delta_detrended, sd, C1/C2/C3 stats, decision flags
prereg.txt: frozen estimator, contrasts, thresholds, repeatability rule, kill switches

Report Table (fields)
session_id | block_name | mean_δ̂′ (×10⁻¹²) | sd_δ̂′ (×10⁻¹²) | C1 Δ, p | C2 Δ, p | C3 Δ, p | r_recentered | pass/fail

# Session 2 — Paper Run: ψ-Modulated Clock Drift (synthetic, prereg-style)
# 
# What this script does:
# - Simulates 5 blocks × 30 min at 1 Hz around 10 MHz: A (Control), B (Activ@X), C (Sham@X), D (Activ@Y), A' (Control after swap)
# - Adds realistic nuisances: common thermal drift (slow), phase random walk, a few glitches per block
# - Uses robust Huber IRLS to fit slope on unwrapped phase per block and per 6× five-minute windows
# - Detrends by subtracting the average Control slope (A and A')
# - Computes contrasts (C1: X−Sham one-sided greater; C2: X−Y one-sided greater; C3: (X+Y)/2−Control two-sided)
# - Performs paired permutation tests over window slopes
# - Blind labels the blocks (randomized order) and saves both blinded and unblinded artifacts
#
# Outputs in: /mnt/data/clock_paper_run/session2/
# - *_timeseries.csv (phase vs time), *_phase_fit.png (fit plots)
# - blinded_block_summary.csv, blind_map.json, unblinded_block_stats.csv, minimal_report.json
#
# Plotting constraints: matplotlib only, one plot per figure, no explicit colors set.

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from pathlib import Path
import json

# -----------------------------
# Parameters
# -----------------------------
root = Path("/mnt/data/clock_paper_run/session2")
root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

seed = 20250811 # reproducible session seed
rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

f_hz = 10_000_000.0 # 10 MHz
block_seconds = 30*60 # 30 minutes
fs = 1.0 # 1 Hz sampling
N = int(block_seconds * fs) # samples per block
t = np.arange(N) / fs # seconds

# Target signals (fractional frequency offsets)
delta = {
"A_Control": 0.0,
"B_ActivX": +1e-11,
"C_ShamX": 0.0,
"D_ActivY": -1e-11,
"A2_Control": 0.0
}

# Nuisance model parameters
common_linear_drift = rng.normal(0.0, 3e-12) # small residual linear drift shared across blocks
sin_amp = rng.normal(0.0, 1.5e-12) # slow sin wobbles (e.g., ambient)
sin_freq = 1.0/(12*60) # ~12 min period
rw_sigma = 0.005 # rad/step for random walk (phase)
white_sigma = 0.02 # white phase noise (rad)
n_glitches = 3 # glitches per block
glitch_amp = 0.8 # rad jump size (approx)

# Robust fitting params
huber_k = 1.345
max_iter = 20

# Windows (6× five-minute)
win_len = int(5*60*fs)
n_win = 6

# -----------------------------
# Helpers
# -----------------------------
def gen_phase_trace(delta_frac, rng):
# Deterministic slope from fractional offset (rad/s)
slope = 2*np.pi * f_hz * delta_frac # rad/s
# Common thermal-like drift (shared across blocks)
thermal = 2*np.pi * f_hz * (common_linear_drift * t) + 2*np.pi * f_hz * (sin_amp * np.sin(2*np.pi*sin_freq*t)/ (2*np.pi*sin_freq*t.size if False else 1.0))
# Base phase
phi = slope * t + thermal
# Random walk
rw = np.cumsum(rng.normal(0.0, rw_sigma, size=t.shape[0]))
# White phase noise
white = rng.normal(0.0, white_sigma, size=t.shape[0])
# Glitches (steps)
phi = phi + rw + white
idxs = rng.choice(len(t), size=n_glitches, replace=False)
idxs.sort()
for idx in idxs:
step = rng.choice([-1,1]) * (glitch_amp + 0.2*rng.normal())
phi[idx:] += step
return phi

def robust_line_slope(y, x=None, k=1.345, max_iter=20):
# Huber IRLS for slope-only (with intercept)
if x is None:
x = np.arange(len(y), dtype=float)
x = x.astype(float)
y = y.astype(float)
X = np.column_stack([np.ones_like(x), x])
# Initial ordinary least squares
beta = np.linalg.lstsq(X, y, rcond=None)[0]
for _ in range(max_iter):
resid = y - X @ beta
mad = np.median(np.abs(resid - np.median(resid))) + 1e-12
s = 1.4826 * mad
if s == 0:
w = np.ones_like(resid)
else:
r = resid / s
w = np.where(np.abs(r) <= k, 1.0, k/np.abs(r))
W = np.diag(w)
beta_new = np.linalg.lstsq(W @ X, W @ y, rcond=None)[0]
if np.allclose(beta_new, beta, rtol=1e-6, atol=1e-9):
beta = beta_new
break
beta = beta_new
intercept, slope = beta[0], beta[1]
return slope, intercept

def block_fit_and_windows(phase):
# Block-level robust slope
slope_block, intercept_block = robust_line_slope(phase, t)
# Windows
slopes_win = []
for i in range(n_win):
s = i*win_len
e = s + win_len
sl, _ = robust_line_slope(phase[s:e], t[s:e])
slopes_win.append(sl)
return slope_block, np.array(slopes_win)

def paired_perm_pvalue(diff_vec, n_perm=20000, alternative="greater", rng=None):
# Paired sign-flip permutation test on differences
rng = rng or np.random.default_rng()
diff = np.asarray(diff_vec)
obs = diff.mean()
# Sign flips
signs = rng.choice([-1,1], size=(n_perm, diff.size))
perm_means = (signs * diff).mean(axis=1)
if alternative == "greater":
p = (np.sum(perm_means >= obs) + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
elif alternative == "less":
p = (np.sum(perm_means <= obs) + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
else: # two-sided
p = (np.sum(np.abs(perm_means) >= abs(obs)) + 1) / (n_perm + 1)
return float(obs), float(p)

# -----------------------------
# Simulate blocks
# -----------------------------
blocks = list(delta.keys()) # fixed logical order
# Randomize a blind order
blind_labels = [f"blk_{h:04x}" for h in rng.integers(0, 65535, size=len(blocks))]
perm = rng.permutation(len(blocks))

blind_map = {blind_labels[i]: blocks[perm[i]] for i in range(len(blocks))}
inv_blind_map = {v: k for k, v in blind_map.items()}

# Generate data
phase_by_block = {}
for name in blocks:
phase = gen_phase_trace(delta[name], rng)
phase_by_block[name] = phase

# Save timeseries and plots under blind labels
for name in blocks:
blind = inv_blind_map[name]
df = pd.DataFrame({"time_s": t, "phase_rad": phase_by_block[name]})
df.to_csv(root / f"{blind}_timeseries.csv", index=False)
# Fit and plot (block-level)
slope_block, _ = robust_line_slope(df["phase_rad"].values, df["time_s"].values, k=huber_k, max_iter=max_iter)
fit_line = (df["time_s"].values * slope_block) + (df["phase_rad"].values[0])
plt.figure(figsize=(7,4))
plt.plot(df["time_s"].values, df["phase_rad"].values)
plt.plot(df["time_s"].values, fit_line)
plt.title(f"{blind}: Phase vs Time with Robust Fit")
plt.xlabel("Time (s)")
plt.ylabel("Phase (rad)")
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig(root / f"{blind}_phase_fit.png", dpi=150)
plt.show()

# -----------------------------
# Robust slope extraction (block + windows)
# -----------------------------
slopes_block = {}
slopes_windows = {}

for name in blocks:
sl_block, sl_win = block_fit_and_windows(phase_by_block[name])
slopes_block[name] = sl_block
slopes_windows[name] = sl_win

# Convert slopes to fractional deltas
def slope_to_delta_frac(slope_rad_per_s):
return slope_rad_per_s / (2*np.pi*f_hz)

deltas_block = {k: slope_to_delta_frac(v) for k, v in slopes_block.items()}
deltas_windows = {k: slope_to_delta_frac(v) for k, v in slopes_windows.items()}

# -----------------------------
# Detrend (subtract mean Control from A & A')
# -----------------------------
control_windows = np.concatenate([deltas_windows["A_Control"], deltas_windows["A2_Control"]])
control_mean = control_windows.mean()

detrended_windows = {k: v - control_mean for k, v in deltas_windows.items()}
detrended_block = {k: v - control_mean for k, v in deltas_block.items()}

# -----------------------------
# Blinded summary table (per block & per window) 
# -----------------------------
# Store only blind labels here
blinded_rows = []
for name in blocks:
blind = inv_blind_map[name]
row = {
"blind_label": blind,
"block_length_s": block_seconds,
"window_len_s": win_len,
"delta_block_frac_detrended": float(detrended_block[name])
}
# add window values
for i, v in enumerate(detrended_windows[name], start=1):
row[f"win{i}_delta_frac_detrended"] = float(v)
blinded_rows.append(row)

blinded_df = pd.DataFrame(blinded_rows)
blinded_df.to_csv(root / "blinded_block_summary.csv", index=False)

with open(root / "blind_map.json", "w") as f:
json.dump(blind_map, f, indent=2)

# -----------------------------
# Unblinded block stats
# -----------------------------
unblind_rows = []
for name in blocks:
unblind_rows.append({
"block": name,
"delta_block_frac_raw": float(deltas_block[name]),
"delta_block_frac_detrended": float(detrended_block[name])
})
unblinded_df = pd.DataFrame(unblind_rows)
unblinded_df.to_csv(root / "unblinded_block_stats.csv", index=False)

# -----------------------------
# Contrasts over windows (paired permutation tests)
# -----------------------------
rng2 = np.random.default_rng(seed+999)

X = detrended_windows["B_ActivX"]
Y = detrended_windows["D_ActivY"]
Sh = detrended_windows["C_ShamX"]
Ctl = np.concatenate([detrended_windows["A_Control"], detrended_windows["A2_Control"]])
Ctl = Ctl.reshape(2, -1).mean(axis=0) # average A and A' window-wise

# Ensure equal lengths
assert X.size == Y.size == Sh.size == Ctl.size == n_win

# C1: X - Sham (one-sided greater)
c1_diff = X - Sh
c1_stat, c1_p = paired_perm_pvalue(c1_diff, n_perm=20000, alternative="greater", rng=rng2)

# C2: X - Y (one-sided greater; expects X positive vs Y negative → big difference)
c2_diff = X - Y
c2_stat, c2_p = paired_perm_pvalue(c2_diff, n_perm=20000, alternative="greater", rng=rng2)

# C3: (X+Y)/2 - Control (two-sided ≈ 0)
c3_diff = 0.5*(X + Y) - Ctl
c3_stat, c3_p = paired_perm_pvalue(c3_diff, n_perm=20000, alternative="two-sided", rng=rng2)

# -----------------------------
# Minimal report
# -----------------------------
report = {
"session_seed": seed,
"f_hz": f_hz,
"block_seconds": block_seconds,
"window_len_s": win_len,
"blind_map_path": str(root / "blind_map.json"),
"blinded_summary_path": str(root / "blinded_block_summary.csv"),
"unblinded_block_stats_path": str(root / "unblinded_block_stats.csv"),
"contrasts": {
"C1_X_minus_Sham": {"mean_diff_frac": float(c1_stat), "p_value_one_sided": float(c1_p)},
"C2_X_minus_Y": {"mean_diff_frac": float(c2_stat), "p_value_one_sided": float(c2_p)},
"C3_avgXY_minus_Control": {"mean_diff_frac": float(c3_stat), "p_value_two_sided": float(c3_p)}
},
"decision": {
"SOI_threshold_abs_delta": 3e-12,
"block_estimates_frac": {k: float(v) for k, v in detrended_block.items()},
"criteria": {
"abs_X_ge_SOI": abs(detrended_block["B_ActivX"]) >= 3e-12,
"abs_Y_ge_SOI": abs(detrended_block["D_ActivY"]) >= 3e-12,
"sign_flip": np.sign(detrended_block["B_ActivX"]) == -np.sign(detrended_block["D_ActivY"]),
"sham_near_zero": abs(detrended_block["C_ShamX"]) <= 1e-12,
"pvals": {"C1<0.01": c1_p < 0.01, "C2<0.01": c2_p < 0.01, "C3_ns": c3_p > 0.05}
}
}
}
with open(root / "minimal_report.json", "w") as f:
json.dump(report, f, indent=2)

# -----------------------------
# Also save human-readable condition order
# -----------------------------
order_df = pd.DataFrame({"blind_label": list(blind_map.keys()), "condition": list(blind_map.values())})
order_df.to_csv(root / "blind_label_to_condition.csv", index=False)

# Show the unblinded table to the user
from ace_tools import display_dataframe_to_user
display_dataframe_to_user("Session 2 — Unblinded Block Stats (δ̂, detrended)", unblinded_df)

# Provide paths for downloads
print({
"dir": str(root),
"blinded_summary_csv": str(root / "blinded_block_summary.csv"),
"blind_map_json": str(root / "blind_map.json"),
"unblinded_block_stats_csv": str(root / "unblinded_block_stats.csv"),
"minimal_report_json": str(root / "minimal_report.json")
})
